Let me think: Coq's generated induction principle replaces recursive occurrences with P. For `SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t') -> strongly_normalizing t`, the induction principle is:

I believe Coq replaces the recursive occurrence `strongly_normalizing t'` with `P t'` even inside a forall in a premise. But wait, the outer argument is a function taking the "forall t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'". For induction, Coq processes premises: it adds IHs for premises that are recursive occurrences or contain them as conclusions? The rule: the induction principle includes an induction hypothesis for each premise whose type is a recursive occurrence, or is a function type with recursive occurrence(s) in the conclusion — specifically when the premises are of the form `forall x, ... -> I ...`. Hmm, actually the general rule: for a constructor argument of type `forall x1..xn, I params args` (a recursive occurrence as conclusion), the IH is `forall x1..xn, P args`. For a premise not of that form (like `step t t'`, not recursive), no IH.

Here the constructor argument itself is the premise `(forall t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t')` which is a product ending in a recursive occurrence: `forall t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'`. Coq treats this as an "inductive premise" of the form `forall x, P -> I` and generates IH `forall t', step t t' -> P t'`. Yes I'm fairly confident that's how it works.

Define:

```
Definition sn (t : tm) := strongly_normalizing t.
```

Standard "hereditarily SN" (HSN) relation on closed-ish terms. Since our terms include fvars and the step relation always requires locally_closed arguments in various places, let's think about what facts we need.

The fundamental theorem: for all Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> for all sigma, (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)) -> R T (subst sigma t).

But our language lacks a general substitution function; it has open_rec which substitutes one term for a de Bruijn index. We can define substitution of fvars by a sigma: `Fixpoint subst_f (s : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm` replacing tm_fvar x with s x. We're allowed to add definitions in Task.v. So define our own substitution. That's fine.

Alternatively use the standard locally nameless approach: the "open" operation used in T_Abs means we can do the fundamental theorem by induction on has_type, where T_Abs case needs "for all x fresh, R T2 (open t1 (fvar x))" implies R (T1->T2) (abs T1 t1) — this requires relating open t1 (fvar x) to app (abs T1 t1) v for a value v of type T1. That's where the substitution lemma (opening with fvar x and then substituting x := v equals open t1 v) comes in. And the "forall x fresh" quantified statements need picking fresh atoms.

This is the standard locally nameless pain. Using a sigma-based substitution might be cleaner: prove fundamental theorem with explicit substitution `subst_f sigma t` where sigma maps atoms to terms. Then:

- T_Var: subst_f sigma (fvar x) = sigma x, use the hypothesis.

Actually there's a subtlety in the "open with fvar then substituting" lemma: consider t1 = tm_bvar 1 (a dangling bvar, locally closed at 1 means bvars < 1... bvar 1 is not lc at 1 but could be lc at 2). open t1 (fvar x) = bvar 1 (unchanged). So the lemma requires x fresh w.r.t. the term... but bvar 0 isn't a fvar! The freshness condition "x not in L" only concerns free variables (fvars) of the body. For ill-scoped bodies like `tm_abs T (tm_bvar 0)`, the standard lemmas indeed fail, unless we restrict to locally closed terms.

Let's think about what properties R needs. Standard Girard-Tait for call-by-value (a.k.a. "strongly normalizing logical relation for CBV"):

For CBV, values are special. Define:
- `R Bool t := sn t` — hmm, but should Bool include only true/false at the "value" level?

The standard approach (e.g., in Harper's PFPL or the "Tait's method for CBV"): define two relations, `V T v` (values) and `E T t` (expressions/terms), with `E T t := sn t /\ (t -->* v -> V T v)` i.e., all terminal forms are values in V, or `E T t := sn t /\ exists v, t -->* v /\ V T v`. For strong normalization, we want a "hereditarily strongly normalizing" relation; the classical approach:

- `SN t := Acc step t`.
- `V Bool v := (v = true \/ v = false)`
- `V (T1->T2) v := v = abs T1' t /\ for all u, V T1 u -> E T2 (app v u)`.
- `E T t := SN t /\ forall t', t -->* t' -> (is a value? ... )` or simply `SN t /\ forall v, t -->* v -> V T v`.

Then E is what the fundamental theorem gives for typable terms; and E implies SN. Closure under reduction expansions comes from the SN part and the "all multisets" part.

This is the classical "reducibility candidates for CBV" (like in the Twelf/PL literature). It's doable but requires multistep reasoning and determinism (for showing E closed under expansion: if t --> t' and E t' then E t: SN t' implies all successors SN; t --> t' one successor — need all successors of t to be SN; if t --> t'' then by determinism t' -->* t'' and E t' gives SN t''. So determinism is needed).

Alternatively, there's a simpler "unary logical relation" for CBV strong normalization used in SF's Norm.v. Let me try hard to recall it. I'm fairly certain SF2's Norm.v (chapter "Norm" in Programming Language Foundations, volume 2) proves exactly `normalization : forall t T, empty |-- t \in T -> sn t` and the approach is:

```
(** ** The Logical Relation *)

(** We need a relation that is closed under reduction... *)
Inductive R : ty -> tm -> Prop :=
  | R_Bool : ...
```

Hmm. Alternatively, I recall the SF chapter uses the "step-indexed" relation `R T t`:

Standard approach (e.g., from "Logical Relations" lecture notes for CBV STLC SN):
- Define `V T v` for *values*: V Bool v := True (or v = true \/ v = false); V (T1->T2) v := forall w, V T1 w -> E T2 (app v w).

Actually for strong normalization with a deterministic CBV semantics, the cleanest is: `E T t := SN t /\ forall v, t -->* v -> V T v`. Then:
- E implies SN.
- If E t' and t --> t', then E t: SN t' → all successors of t' are SN. Need: all successors t'' of t are SN. Since t --> t' and t --> t'', determinism gives t' -->* t''. Then E t' gives SN t''... wait E t' gives SN t' which gives SN of all one-step successors and by induction all multistep successors. So SN t'' from t' -->* t''. And the V part: if t -->* v then t' -->* v so V v. Good, needs determinism.

- app case: Need: E (T1->T2) t1 -> E T1 t2 -> E T2 (app t1 t2). Proof: SN: standard (if t1 SN and t2 SN and ... ). For CBV app, reducing app t1 t2: either t1 steps, or t1 is a value and t2 steps, or both values and t1 = abs, then beta. Standard nested induction on SN t1 and SN t2 with determinism.

Actually, I recall now with more confidence: SF2 `Norm.v` proves normalization for STLC CBV using the following logical relation (I'm reconstructing from the structure of the chapter):

```
(** ** The Logical Relation *)

Definition sn (t : tm) : Prop := strongly_normalizing t.

(* A term is in R T if it is sn and ... *)
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Bool => sn t
  | T1 -> T2 => sn t /\
       forall t', t --> t' -> R (T1->T2) t' /\   (* maybe not *)
       forall t1, R T1 t1 -> R T2 (app t t1)
  end.
```

Let me use the R-as-Fixpoint formulation: R Bool t := SN t; R (T1->T2) t := SN t /\ (forall v value, R T1 v -> R T2 (app t v)). No "value reducts in V" clause. Then for R T2 (app v1 t2), we need SN and app-closure. So the SN part is what I described; the app-closure part for T2 = T3->T4: forall w value R T3 w, R T4 (app (app v1 t2) w). Hmm, we'd prove by using the same lemma at type T2? Circular: we're proving by induction on SN t2 with motive "forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app v1 t2)"; for the app-closure component at T2 we need R T4 (app (app v1 t2) w) which is R T2' (app (app v1 t2) w) — that's the same motive but at a smaller type (T4 < T2? T2 = T3->T4 is not smaller... wait T2 is the type at which we're proving; T4 is a subterm of T2, so we can use induction on the type structure, i.e., a separate mutual induction on types).

OK so it seems we need a simultaneous induction on both the type and the SN derivations. This is the standard "logical relation" proof and it's inherently a bit involved. Let's set it up as:

Main lemma (by induction on type T, with sub-induction on SN):
For all T:
  (1) R T t -> SN t.   [definitional for Bool; for arrow, included]
  (2) R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.   [forward closure]
  (3) R T t' -> t --> t' -> R T t.   [backward closure, uses determinism]
  (4) app lemma: R (T1->T2) t1 -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2)  — depends on T2's properties at smaller types.
  
Hmm, note (4) at arrow T2 = (T3->T4) requires (4) at smaller? Let's see how we prove (4):

Prove (4) by: outer induction on SN t1 with motive P(t1) := R (T1->T2) t1 -> forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2)? Hmm, but actually we might not need an induction on SN t1 at all in the arrow-type case; the SN part needs it. Let's think about the structure of "R T (app t1 t2)" where T = T2:
Components:
  - SN (app t1 t2)
  - if T2 = T3 -> T4: forall w value R T3 w, R T4 (app (app t1 t2) w).
  
Proof of SN (app t1 t2): induction on SN t1 and SN t2 (nested). Standard.

Proof of the second component: given w value with R T3 w, we need R T4 (app (app t1 t2) w). By the app lemma at "intermediate type" — app t1 t2 has type T2 = T3->T4, and applying it to w: that's exactly the app lemma at type T2 with the term (app t1 t2) and argument w: R T2 (app t1 t2) [what we're proving] and R T3 w ⊢ R T4 (app (app t1 t2) w). This is the same lemma at the same type T2 — circular! Hmm. Unless we use the SN-of-term induction: the app lemma with outer induction on SN t1 gives for the "second component" R T4 (app (app t1 t2) w): we can use the outer IH if (app t1 t2) is a reduct... no.

Hmm. Wait. Maybe we should apply the app lemma at the type T2 = T3->T4 differently: to prove R T4 (app (app t1 t2) w), use that app (app t1 t2) w = app t1' t2' where... no.

Standard solution: the app lemma is proven by induction on SN t1 where the IH gives for all t1' smaller (one-step reducts) the full app lemma, AND in the value case, an inner induction on SN t2 with a *different* motive: for the value case t1 = v1 (a value), prove forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app v1 t2) by induction on SN t2. In this inner induction:
  - SN part as described.
  - Arrow-closure part: forall w value R T3 w, R T4 (app (app v1 t2) w). By the inner IH at t2? No, (app v1 t2) isn't a reduct of t2.
  
  Hmm, but here's a trick: use the beta/App2 structure: if T2 = T3 -> T4 and we need closure under app (app v1 t2) w, note that (app v1 t2) is the term; we could apply the app lemma at type T2 with first argument (app v1 t2), which requires R (T3->T4) (app v1 t2) — the very thing being proven. Circular.

Standard Tait proof handles this by defining R on values V and expressions E, where E's arrow case is *defined* as: E (T1->T2) t := SN t /\ forall t', t -->* t' -> value t' -> V (T1->T2) t' — i.e., instead of requiring app-closure of t itself, require that all value-reducts are in V. Then the app lemma doesn't have a recursive "app of app" component: it suffices to show all value-reducts of app t1 t2 are in V. And value-reducts of app t1 t2 are reachable through: reductions of t1, t2, and beta. This seems much cleaner!

Let's define:
- `V Bool v := True` (for values; or specifically v = true ∨ v = false; all values of Bool type are true/false anyway once typed, but semantically we can set V Bool v := True for any value v).
- `V (T1->T2) (abs T t) := forall w, V T1 w -> E T2 (open t w)`; and False for other values.
  Hmm, note we must handle the poorly-scoped abs: open t w is fine.
- `E T t := SN t /\ (forall t', t -->* t' -> value t' -> V T t')`.
  Actually SN t already implies that reduction sequences from t can't go forever, but reachable values exist e.g. t itself if t is a value.
- `V T v` should imply E T v? For values v: SN v holds (values are SN); and value-reducts of v: if v -->* v' with v' value, then since values don't step, v' = v, so V T v holds. ✓.
- `E T t -> SN t`: by definition. ✓.

Now key lemmas:
(A) E T t -> SN t. ✓ def.
(B) Determinism: step t t' -> step t t'' -> t' = t''.
(C) If E T t and step t t' then E T t': SN from SN; value reducts: t' -->* t'' implies t -->* t'' so V. ✓ (no determinism needed).
(D) If E T t' and step t t' then E T t: SN: given step t t'', determinism gives t'' = t', so SN t''. Value reducts: t -->* u implies t' -->* u (since t --> t' and determinism... careful: t -->* u starts with t --> t1; determinism gives t1 = t', so t' -->* u). ✓ Uses determinism.
(E) V T v -> E T v. ✓ (as above; use value_no_step: value v -> ~ step v t').
(F) Beta lemma: V (T1->T2) (abs T t) -> V T1 w -> E T2 (open t w). ✓ by definition of V.
(G) App lemma: E (T1->T2) t1 -> E T1 t2 -> E T2 (app t1 t2).

Let's prove (G). Write E (T1->T2) t1 = SN t1 ∧ (∀ t1' value-reduct: V (T1->T2) t1').
Need E T2 (app t1 t2): SN (app t1 t2) and value-reducts.

For SN: show SN (app t1 t2) by induction on SN t1, then inside on SN t2? Let's define the proof as: 
`Lemma sn_app : forall t1 t2, E (T1->T2) t1 -> E T1 t2 -> SN (app t1 t2)` 
Wait, SN of app might need: every successor SN:
 - App1: t1 --> t1' (lc t2): successor app t1' t2. E at t1' by (C); IH? We're doing induction on SN t1 with motive "forall t2, E T1 t2 -> SN (app t1 t2)"? Then IH applies to t1' with any t2: gives SN (app t1' t2). ✓.
 - App2: value v1 (so t1 = v1 is a value), t2 --> t2', successor app v1 t2'. Need SN. Here t1 is a value so we know V (T1->T2) t1 from E's value-reduct property (t1 -->* t1 by refl, t1 value). We need an inner induction on SN t2 with motive "forall t2, E T1 t2 -> ... app v1 t2". Note E T1 t2' from (C). But the inner induction is on SN t2 and the IH applies to t2' (step t2 t2'): gives SN (app v1 t2') provided we have the appropriate statement for t2'. So we need a lemma: `forall v1, V (T1->T2) v1 -> SN v1 -> (forall t2, E T1 t2 -> SN (app v1 t2))`, proven by induction on SN t2. But wait, the outer induction on SN t1 with motive P t1 := E (T1->T2) t1 -> forall t2, E T1 t2 -> SN (app t1 t2) — in the App2 case, t1 = v1 is a value and the IH of the outer induction gives nothing useful (no reducts of t1). So we need to prove, for a value v1 with V (T1->T2) v1, the inner statement by induction on SN t2. This inner induction is independent of the outer one, so we can do it as a separate lemma. But careful: the inner induction's motive at t2 must be usable at t2' — yes.

   Inner: induction on SN t2 with motive Q t2 := E T1 t2 -> SN (app v1 t2), where v1 fixed value with V (T1->T2) v1.
   - Successor cases of app v1 t2:
     * App1: needs step v1 — impossible (value).
     * App2: t2 --> t2' (value v1), successor app v1 t2'. IH t2' (from SN t2 via step) applied to E T1 t2' (from (C)). ✓
     * Beta: this requires app v1 t2 to be app (abs T t) w with v1 = abs T t and t2 = w a value. Then successor = open t w. Need SN (open t w). We have E T2 (open t w) from (F) V (T1->T2) v1 and V T1 w. So SN. But we need V T1 w: t2 is a value and E T1 t2 gives value-reducts: t2 -->* t2 (refl), value t2 → V T1 t2. ✓
     Also ST_AppAbs requires lc (abs T t): value v1 gives lc. ✓ 
     Hmm, wait: to derive SN (open t w) from the step, since SN is defined as "all successors SN" — from SN (app v1 t2) and step → SN(open t w); but we're *constructing* SN (app v1 t2) here, so we can't invert. We just directly use E T2 (open t w) → SN. ✓.

   So SN (app v1 t2) provable. But note this inner lemma needs to handle *all* t2 (not just E ones): fine.

Now for the value-reduct condition of E T2 (app t1 t2): for all u, app t1 t2 -->* u -> value u -> V T2 u.

We need: any value reachable from app t1 t2 is in V T2. Given E T1 t2 and E (T1->T2) t1 (so SN t1, SN t2). Let's reason: reachable value u. The reduction sequence: reduce t1 to a value v1 (if ever), reduce t2 to v2, then beta → open t v2 → ... Could the sequence reduce t1 partway then...? In CBV, app t1 t2 reduces: while t1 is not a value, step t1; once t1 is a value, step t2; once both values, beta (if t1 abs). So a multistep sequence from app t1 t2 to a value u must go through app v1 v2 with v1, v2 values (reachable value reducts of t1, t2 resp.), then to open t v2, then further reductions of open t v2 to u.

Hmm, but does every multistep sequence have this shape? The rules allow interleaving? ST_App1 reduces t1 regardless of t2 (as long as t2 is lc). So yes, t1 reduces first while not a value; once t1 is a value, only App2 applies (and beta when t2 is a value). So the sequence from app t1 t2: t1's reductions happen first (any number, but SN ensures termination), then t2's, then beta, then open t v2 reduces to u.

We need: V T2 u. This requires knowing that V T2 is closed under multistep reductions from open t v2 to u. Hmm, we know E T2 (open t v2) (from V arrow), and E T2 gives value reducts: u reachable and value → V T2 u. But that's for open t v2, not app t1 t2.

So we need a more general "value-reduct lemma": if app t1 t2 -->* u with u a value, then u is a value-reduct of open t v2 for some reachable values v1=abs T t, v2. Hmm. Alternatively prove the value-reduct condition directly by induction on the multistep derivation:

`Lemma E_app_value : forall t1 t2 u, E (T1->T2) t1 -> E T1 t2 -> app t1 t2 -->* u -> value u -> V T2 u.`

Proof by induction on the multistep derivation? The first step determines the shape. Let's do induction on the multistep: multi_step (app t1 t2) s' rest. Cases on the step:
- App1: app t1 t2 --> app t1' t2 using t1 --> t1', lc t2. Then IH with E arrow t1' (via C) and E T1 t2 gives V T2 u. ✓
- App2: value v1, t2 --> t2', so app v1 t2 --> app v1 t2'. Here t1 = v1 is a value. So E (T1->T2) v1 gives V (T1->T2) v1 (value-reduct refl). IH with E T1 t2' (via C). ✓ (works since t1 = v1).
- Beta: t1 = abs T t (value), t2 = w value, lc, app --> open t w. Then the rest: open t w -->* u, value u. We have E T2 (open t w) from (F) with V T1 w (from E T1 w and value w). Then E T2's value-reduct condition applied to the multistep rest gives V T2 u. ✓

This works beautifully! It requires care: in the App1 case, IH is on the sub-derivation `app t1' t2 -->* u`, yes (multi_step constructor has the rest as an argument, so induction works).

So (G) reduces to: SN (app t1 t2) [via outer/inner inductions] + value-reduct property [via induction on multistep as above].

```
Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with [] => 0 | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs) end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall L x, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof. induction L; simpl; intros; destruct H; subst; [apply Nat.le_max_l | apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list L); auto; apply Nat.le_max_r]. Qed.

Lemma maximum_exists : forall L, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof. intros L; exists (S (max_list L)); intro H; apply max_list_spec in H; apply Nat.nle_succ_diag_l...
```

Note: in `subst_f (extend rho0 x w) (open t1 (fvar x))`, any fvar y ≠ x in t1 maps to rho0 y; the inserted fvar x maps to w. So indeed equal to substituting rho0 in t1 and then replacing bvar 0 by w, IF x ∉ fvars(t1) (so the only fvar x occurrences are the inserted one) and rho0 doesn't introduce bvars that get captured. ✓

OK so now let's also check the sem_env requirement in the T_Abs case: we need `sem_env (x |-> T1 ; Gamma) (extend rho x w)`, i.e.:
  - pointwise lc: extend rho x w y = if y = x then w else rho y; w lc (from V T1 w which gives value → lc) and rho pointwise lc. ✓
  - env typing: for all y S, (x|->T1;Gamma) y = Some S -> E S (extend rho x w y).
    * y = x: S = T1, extend = w, need E T1 w: from V T1 w → E T1 w. ✓
    * y ≠ x: Gamma y = Some S, extend = rho y, from sem_env rho. ✓
    Need to handle the `if Nat.eqb x y` unfolding: `update Gamma x T y = if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y`. Note the definition is `fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y`. With x = y, eqb x x = true. ✓ Use Nat.eqb_eq.

Now the value-reduct property for E (T1->T2) (abs T1 (subst_f rho t1)): 
   For u with abs -->* u and value u: since abs has no steps, u = abs (by induction on multistep: the first step is impossible). Then V (T1->T2) (abs ...): need exists, take T1, subst_f rho t1, and forall w V T1 w -> E T2 (open (subst_f rho t1) w). ✓ as above.

Now let me also double check the E definition's value-reduct clause: `E T t := SN t /\ forall u, t -->* u -> value u -> V T u`. 

Hmm, one subtlety: Should it be "value u" or should V itself include value? Since V T u implies value u (by definition for Bool we set V Bool u := value u; for arrow also include value). Let's keep `value u` explicit for clarity? It doesn't hurt. Hmm, it could help the proof. Let's define:

```
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => value t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      value t /\
      exists T' t1, t = tm_abs T' t1 /\ (forall w, V T1 w -> E T2 (open t1 w))
  end
with E (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall u, multi step t u -> value u -> V T u).
```
Mutual fixpoint on T — Coq allows mutual Fixpoint with the same decreasing argument T; E refers to V T (same T) and V refers to E T2 (smaller). Structural recursion should be acceptable: in V arrow, E T2 with T2 a subterm of T. In E T, V T same argument — for mutual fixpoints, need the recursive calls to be on structurally smaller arguments; E T calls V T (same) — is that allowed in mutual fixpoint? In a mutual block, calls between the two functions must be on a structurally smaller argument than the *current* function's decreasing argument. Hmm: the guard condition for mutual fixpoints requires recursive calls (including mutual ones) to have a structurally smaller argument than the caller's decreasing argument? I believe in a mutual fixpoint, a call from f to g must decrease the argument relative to f's decreasing argument. So E T calling V T with the same T would fail the guard.

Fix: define E in terms of V without recursion on T: `E T t := SN t /\ forall u, multi step t u -> value u -> V T u`. Here E is a *definition* using V at the same T. If we define V by Fixpoint and E as a Definition referring to V, that's fine (V is defined; E T uses V T, no recursion issue since E isn't recursive). But V's arrow case refers to E T2, and E refers to V — circular between V and E! Since E is defined after V, V can't reference E. Unless we inline E in V:

```
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => value t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      value t /\ exists T' t1, t = tm_abs T' t1 /\
        (forall w, V T1 w ->
           strongly_normalizing (open t1 w) /\
           (forall u, multi step (open t1 w) u -> value u -> V T2 u))
  end.
```
Inlining E's definition. This is structurally recursive on T. ✓ Then define:
`Definition E (T : ty) (t : tm) := strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall u, multi step t u -> value u -> V T u).`
Fine.

Definitions:

```coq
Definition sn (t : tm) : Prop := strongly_normalizing t.

Fixpoint V (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => value t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      value t /\
      (exists T' t1, t = tm_abs T' t1 /\
         (forall w, V T1 w ->
            strongly_normalizing (open t1 w) /\
            (forall u, multi step (open t1 w) u -> value u -> V T2 u)))
  end.

Definition E (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall u, multi step t u -> value u -> V T u).
```

Hmm, `open t1 w` where t1 is the body. Fine.

Need lemmas:

1. `value_no_step : forall v t, value v -> ~ step v t`.
   Proof by inversion on value and step. Cases: v = abs T t0 with lc; step (abs ...) t' impossible (inversion on step: all constructors conclude app or if). v = true/false similarly. 
   Actually: `intros v t Hv. induction Hv; intros Hstep; inversion Hstep; subst; ...` Let's see: step constructors produce tm_app or tm_if, and value constructors produce abs/true/false, so inversion on step should fail (no unifiable constructor). We might need to handle the case where inversion leaves goals. Let's write:
   ```
   Lemma value_no_step : forall v t, value v -> ~ step v t.
   Proof.
     intros v t Hv. induction Hv; intro Hstep; inversion Hstep.
   Qed.
   ```
   For v_abs: goal False with Hstep : step (tm_abs T t) t'. inversion Hstep: constructors ST_AppAbs (conclusion tm_app ...) etc. — `inversion Hstep` should solve since no constructor matches (Coq's inversion on impossible cases with different constructors: since the conclusion of Hstep is `step (tm_abs T t) t'`, and no step constructor has that conclusion form (all are app or if), inversion should close the goal). Hmm, but ST_AppAbs concludes `tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v`; unifying `tm_app ... = tm_abs ...` fails. Coq's `inversion` should derive False from the discrimination. I think `inversion Hstep` will work, though sometimes you need `inversion Hstep; subst; discriminate` or `inversion Hstep; congruence`. We'll test.

2. `sn_value : forall v, value v -> strongly_normalizing v`. Since no steps from v: `intro; constructor; intros t' H; exfalso; eapply value_no_step; eauto.`

3. Determinism: 
   ```
   Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
   ```
   Proof by induction on the first step derivation, inversion on the second. Cases:

   - ST_App2: value v1, t2 --> t2'. Second: ST_App2 with IH; ST_App1 needs step v1 — contradiction (value).
   - ST_IfTrue t1 t2 lc lc: t = if true t1 t2; result t1. Second: ST_IfTrue (same), ST_IfFalse needs true = false (discriminate), ST_If needs step true — contradiction.
   - ST_IfFalse similar.
   - ST_If: t1 --> t1'; second ST_If with IH; ST_IfTrue/False need t1 = true/false but step t1 t1' contradicts value_no_step (true/false are values).
   
   Proving this will require some case work with inversion; doable.

Now the fundamental theorem.

```
Definition sem_env (Gamma : context) (rho : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (rho x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> E T (rho x)).
```

`subst_f : (atom -> tm) -> tm -> tm`, defined by recursion:
```
Fixpoint subst_f (rho : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => rho x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_f rho t1) (subst_f rho t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_f rho t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst_f rho t1) (subst_f rho t2) (subst_f rho t3)
  end.
```

Fundamental theorem:
```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall rho, sem_env Gamma rho -> E T (subst_f rho t).
Proof.
  induction 1; intros rho [Hlc Hsem].
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply Hsem. assumption.
  - (* T_Abs *) ...
  - (* T_App *) simpl. apply E_app; [apply IHhas_type1; split|apply IHhas_type2; split]; auto.
  ...
```
Hmm, careful with the induction: `induction 1` on has_type. T_Abs's IH: `forall rho, sem_env (x |-> T1 ; Gamma) rho -> E T2 (subst_f rho (open t1 (fvar x)))` — but this needs to be under the binder `forall x, ~ In x L -> ...`. The induction principle for T_Abs: the constructor has a premise `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`, and Coq generates an IH for each recursive occurrence in the premises. For a premise of the form `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...`, Coq generates IH: `forall x, ~ In x L -> forall rho, sem_env (x|->T1;Gamma) rho -> E T2 (subst_f rho (open t1 (fvar x)))`. I believe Coq does generate the IH with the forall and the non-recursive premise (like ~ In x L) as an assumption. Yes, standard.

Hmm, but the induction statement includes `forall rho, ...` which must be in the motive to get the IH generalized. Let's state it as:
```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T (H : has_type Gamma t T),
  forall rho, sem_env Gamma rho -> E T (subst_f rho t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H.
  induction H; intros rho Hsem.
```
Here the motive is `fun Gamma t T H => forall rho, sem_env Gamma rho -> E T (subst_f rho t)`. Since we intro'd rho after, the IH will be the motive applied. Good.

    ```
    Lemma sn_if : forall t1 t2 t3, SN t1 -> SN t2 -> SN t3 -> SN (if t1 t2 t3).
    Proof.
      intros t1 t2 t3 H1 H2 H3. revert t2 t3 H2 H3.

      constructor. intros u Hstep. inversion Hstep; subst.
    ```

OK. Now the fundamental theorem T_Abs case details:

```
| T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1,
    (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2) ->
    has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)
```
IH: `forall x, ~ In x L -> forall rho, sem_env (update Gamma x T1) rho -> E T2 (subst_f rho (open t1 (fvar x)))`.

Goal: E (T1->T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst_f rho t1)), given Hsem : sem_env Gamma rho.

Unfold E. split.
- SN (tm_abs T1 (subst_f rho t1)): apply sn_abs. ✓
- intros u Hm Hval. 
   From Hm : multi step (abs T1 (subst_f rho t1)) u and Hval : value u.
   Have `abs_multi_eq : multi step (tm_abs T t) u -> u = tm_abs T t` (since no steps from abs). Then subst u.
   Need V (T1->T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst_f rho t1)): 
   split: 
   * lc (tm_abs T1 (subst_f rho t1)): from Hval : value (tm_abs T1 (subst_f rho t1)) → inversion gives lc. ✓ (v_abs's premise). Nice — we get lc from Hval!
   * exists T1, subst_f rho t1; split refl; intros w HVw.
     Need E T2 (open (subst_f rho t1) w).
     - Choose fresh x: not in L and not in fvs t1. We need a `fvs` function or a "free fvars" predicate. Alternatively: is x ∉ fvs(t1) actually needed? Let's think again... 
     
     Hmm! Wait. Do we actually need x fresh w.r.t. t1? The IH gives, for any x ∉ L, E T2 (subst_f (extend rho x w) (open t1 (fvar x))). And we want E T2 (open (subst_f rho t1) w). The commutation identity requires x ∉ fvs(t1). But here's the thing: the typing hypothesis is available for ALL x ∉ L. If t1 happens to contain a particular fvar x, we just pick a different one. So we need ∃ x, x ∉ L ∧ x ∉ fvs t1. Since L is finite and fvs t1 is finite, such x exists.
     
     So I need `fvs : tm -> list atom` (or a predicate with a finite-set lemma). Let me define:
     ```
     Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
       match t with
       | tm_bvar _ => nil
       | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
       | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
       | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
       | tm_true => nil
       | tm_false => nil
       | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
       end.
     ```
     And `fresh_for : forall L, exists x, ~ In x L` via max_list.
     Then pick x fresh for `L ++ fvs t1`.
     
     Then the commutation lemma:
     ```
     Lemma subst_open_comm : forall rho x w t k,
       (forall y, lc_at 0 (rho y)) ->   (* rho pointwise lc *)
       lc_at 0 w ->
       ~ In x (fvs t) ->
       subst_f (extend rho x w) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k w (subst_f rho t).
     ```
     Hmm, "lc_at 0 (rho y)" is `locally_closed (rho y)`. Let me double check the statement with the k parameter.

     t = fvar y (y ≠ x), k arbitrary: LHS: fvar y → rho y.      t = abs T t': LHS: abs T (subst_f (extend rho x w) (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t')). RHS: open_rec k w (abs T (subst_f rho t')) = abs T (open_rec (S k) w (subst_f rho t')). By IH at k := S k ✓ (freshness for t' follows from fvs abs = fvs t').

```
Lemma E_step_bwd : forall T t t', E T t' -> step t t' -> E T t.
Proof.
  intros T t t' [Hsn' Hvr'] Hstep. split.
  - (* SN t *) constructor. intros u Hstep2.

    exact Hsn'.
  - intros u Hm Hval.
    (* t -->* u; derive t' -->* u *)
```

Now, an important detail: the commutation lemma has `~ In x (fvs t)`. But the IH is about `open t1 (fvar x)`, so we need x ∉ fvs t1 — yes.

Let me write the commutation lemma proof:
```
Lemma subst_open_comm : forall rho x w t k,
  (forall y, locally_closed (rho y)) ->
  locally_closed w ->
  ~ In x (fvs t) ->
  subst_f (extend rho x w) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) =
  open_rec k w (subst_f rho t).
Proof.
  intros rho x w t. induction t; intros k Hlc Hw Hfresh; simpl in *.
  - (* bvar n *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + (* n = k *) apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst n. simpl. 
       (* LHS: subst_f (extend rho x w) (tm_fvar x) = extend rho x w x = w *)
       (* RHS: open_rec k w (tm_bvar k) = w *)
       unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.
    + (* n ≠ k *) simpl. (* LHS: subst_f ... (bvar n) = bvar n; RHS: open_rec k w (bvar n) = bvar n *) reflexivity.
  - (* fvar a *) simpl. unfold extend.
    destruct (Nat.eqb x a) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst a. 
      LHS: w. RHS: open_rec k w (rho x) — need lc (rho x) → open_rec identity.
      apply open_rec_lc0; auto.
    + (* a ≠ x *) LHS: rho a. RHS: open_rec k w (rho a) = rho a by lc. apply open_rec_lc0; auto.
  - (* app *) simpl. rewrite IHt1, IHt2; auto. (* fvs app = fvs t1 ++ fvs t2; freshness decomposes *) 
  - (* abs *) simpl. rewrite IHt; auto. simpl in Hfresh; auto.
  - (* true/false *) reflexivity.
  - (* if *) simpl. rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3; auto. 
Qed.
```
Wait, in the bvar case, the term changes with k, and `open_rec k (fvar x) (tm_bvar n)` = if eqb k n then fvar x else bvar n. Then subst_f of that. And `subst_f rho (tm_bvar n) = tm_bvar n`, then open_rec k w (bvar n) = if eqb k n then w else bvar n. So both sides equal (if eqb k n then w else bvar n) ✓.

The fvar case: `open_rec k (fvar x) (fvar a) = fvar a`; then subst_f (extend rho x w) (fvar a) = extend rho x w a = if eqb x a then w else rho a. RHS: subst_f rho (fvar a) = rho a; open_rec k w (rho a). Need: if eqb x a then w else rho a = open_rec k w (rho a). If eqb x a = true, then a = x, then RHS = open_rec k w (rho x) = rho x (by lc of rho x), and LHS = w. NOT EQUAL unless w = rho x! 

Hmm! Wait, but the freshness assumption says x ∉ fvs t. Here t = fvar a and a = x, so x ∈ fvs t, contradicting freshness. So this case is impossible — I need to use Hfresh : ~ In x (fvs (tm_fvar a)) = ~ In x (a :: nil) → x ≠ a. ✓ So in the fvar case, use Hfresh to rule out a = x. Let me redo: 
  - fvar a: destruct (Nat.eqb x a) eqn:E; [apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst a; exfalso; apply Hfresh; simpl; auto|]. Then both sides rho a (using lc rho a) ✓.

Wait, but if eqb x a = false, extend rho x w a = rho a ✓, RHS = open_rec k w (rho a) = rho a by lc ✓. Good.

```
  - simpl in Hin; contradiction.
```

```coq
(* ---------- auxiliary definitions and lemmas ---------- *)

Definition sn (t : tm) : Prop := strongly_normalizing t.

(* free variables of a term *)
Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall L x, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list ys); [apply IH; assumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

  intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). intro Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_spec L (S (max_list L)) Hin).

Qed.
```

```coq
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then w else rho y.

  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
```

Let's think about how Coq handles `induction Hm` when Hm : multi step v u with v, u variables and Hv : value v in context. Coq will revert Hv (it depends on v) and induct with motive `fun x y (h : multi step x y) => value x -> y = x`. Then:

```
Lemma value_multi_eq : forall v u, value v -> multi step v u -> u = v.
Proof.
  intros v u Hv Hm.
  induction Hm as [x | x y z Hstep Hrest IH].

```
In the step case, x = v by unification. Hstep : step x y where x is the original v.
```
Lemma value_multi_eq : forall v u, value v -> multi step v u -> u = v.
```

Wait, `revert v Hv` leaves goal `forall v, value v -> ?` but u is fixed as the endpoint; after revert, the goal is `forall v, value v -> multi step v u -> u = v`? No — Hm : multi step v u was in context; reverting v with Hm depending on it... `revert v Hv` will fail because Hm depends on v? Actually `revert v` reverts v and all hypotheses depending on it, including Hm (if we name it) — but if Hm is in the context, revert v also reverts Hm. Hmm, we want to keep Hm for the induction. Order: intro Hm first, revert v Hv means reverting v (and Hm since it depends on v)... that's wrong.

Simplest robust approach: state the lemma with the value judgment after the multistep:
```
Lemma value_multi_eq : forall v u, multi step v u -> value v -> u = v.
Proof.
  intros v u Hm. induction Hm as [x | x y z Hstep Hrest IH]; intro Hv.
  - reflexivity.
  - exfalso. eapply value_no_step; eassumption.
Qed.
```
Here Hv is introduced after the induction, so the motive is `fun x y h => value x -> y = x`, and the IH is `value y -> z = y`. In the step case, Hv : value x, Hstep : step x y → contradiction ✓. And Coq generalizes x, y over the induction since they're variables ✓. 

Similarly `abs_multi_eq` — I can just use `value_multi_eq` with `value (tm_abs T t)` — but that requires lc! For an ill-scoped abs, value doesn't hold. Hmm, but in the fundamental theorem T_Abs case, I need `abs -->* u` implies u = abs for an ill-scoped abs. So I need the general abs lemma:

```
Lemma abs_multi_eq : forall T t u, multi step (tm_abs T t) u -> u = tm_abs T t.
```
Problem: the compound index. Solution: generalize the statement to use a variable and an equation:
```
Lemma abs_multi_eq : forall v u, multi step v u -> (exists T t, v = tm_abs T t) -> u = v.
Proof.
  intros v u Hm. induction Hm as [x | x y z Hstep Hrest IH]; intros [T [t Hx]].
  - reflexivity.
  - subst x. exfalso. inversion Hstep.
Qed.
```
✓ This works: the induction generalizes v (variable), and the extra hypothesis is introduced after the induction. In the step case: Hx : x = tm_abs T t; subst x; Hstep : step (tm_abs T t) y; inversion Hstep → False ✓.

```
Lemma E_if : forall T t1 t2 t3,
  E Ty_Bool t1 -> E T t2 -> E T t3 -> E T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
Proof.
  intros T t1 t2 t3 HE1 HE2 HE3.
  enough (Hgen : forall t1, strongly_normalizing t1 -> E Ty_Bool t1 ->
     forall t2 t3, E T t2 -> E T t3 -> E T (tm_if t1 t2 t3)).
  { apply Hgen; [exact (proj1 HE1) | exact HE1 | exact HE2 | exact HE3]. }
  clear t1 HE1 HE2 HE3.
  intros t1 Hsn1. induction Hsn1 as [t1 IH]. intros HE1 t2 t3 HE2 HE3.
  apply E_intro.
  - (* SN *) apply sn_if; [ exact (proj1 HE1) | exact (proj1 HE2) | exact (proj1 HE3) ].
  - (* value *) intros Hv; inversion Hv.
  - (* step *) intros u Hstep; inversion Hstep; subst.
    + (* ST_IfTrue *) exact HE2.
    + (* ST_IfFalse *) exact HE3.
    + (* ST_If *) apply IH; [exact Hstep | eapply E_step_fwd; eassumption | exact HE2 | exact HE3].
Qed.
```
✓ (assuming the IH shape).

```
Lemma sn_if : forall t1 t2 t3, SN t1 -> SN t2 -> SN t3 -> SN (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 t3 H1. 
  enough (Hgen : forall t1, SN t1 -> forall t2 t3, SN t2 -> SN t3 -> SN (if t1 t2 t3)).
  { apply Hgen; assumption. }
  clear t1 H1.
  intros t1 H1. induction H1 as [t1 IH]. intros t2 t3 H2 H3.
  constructor. intros u Hstep. inversion Hstep; subst.
  - exact H2.
  - exact H3.
  - apply IH; [exact Hstep | exact H2 | exact H3].
Qed.
```

One thing to double check: the `E` definition referencing `V` and vice versa. I'll inline E into V as discussed:

```
Fixpoint V (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => value t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\
      (exists T' t1, t = tm_abs T' t1 /\
        (forall w, V T1 w ->
           strongly_normalizing (open t1 w) /\
           (forall u, multi step (open t1 w) u -> value u -> V T2 u)))
  end.

Definition E (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall u, multi step t u -> value u -> V T u).
```

```
  V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T' t1) -> V T1 w -> E T2 (open t1 w).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 T' t1 w [_ [T'' [t'' [Heq H]]]] HVw.
  inversion Heq; subst.  (* tm_abs T' t1 = tm_abs T'' t'' → T' = T'', t1 = t'' *)
  apply H; exact HVw.
Qed.
```

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the normalization proof. *)

Definition sn (t : tm) : Prop := strongly_normalizing t.

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall L x, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list ys); [apply IH; assumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). intro Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_spec L (S (max_list L)) Hin) as H.
  apply Nat.nle_succ_diag_l in H. exact H.
Qed.
```

Hmm, `Nat.nle_succ_diag_l` — let me verify the name exists. In Coq's PeanoNat: `nle_succ_diag_l : forall n : nat, ~ S n <= n`. I'm fairly confident. If not, alternative: `apply Nat.lt_irrefl with (n := max_list L)` hmm. Let me have a fallback: `assert (H' : max_list L < S (max_list L)) by apply Nat.lt_succ_diag_r. apply Nat.lt_le_antisym? ` Hmm. Or simply use `lia` if available. Let me check in the environment first.

```coq
(* ===== Added by the proof: auxiliary definitions and lemmas ===== *)

(** ** Free variables, freshness *)

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall L x, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list ys); [apply IH; assumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). intro Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_spec L (S (max_list L)) Hin) as H.
  apply Nat.nle_succ_diag_l in H. exact H.
Qed.

(** ** Substitution of free variables *)

Definition extend (rho : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (w : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then w else rho y.

Fixpoint subst_f (rho : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => rho x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_f rho t1) (subst_f rho t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_f rho t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst_f rho t1) (subst_f rho t2) (subst_f rho t3)
  end.

Lemma subst_f_id : forall t, subst_f (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof. induction t; simpl; congruence. Qed.

Lemma open_rec_lc : forall t n u, lc_at n t -> open_rec n u t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; intros n u Hlc; simpl; auto.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb n n0) eqn:E; auto.
    apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n0.
    inversion Hlc. subst. exfalso. eapply Nat.lt_irrefl. eassumption.
  - inversion Hlc; subst. rewrite IHt1, IHt2; auto.
  - f_equal. apply IHt. inversion Hlc; assumption.
  - inversion Hlc; subst. rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3; auto.
Qed.
```

```coq
(** ** Basic properties of values and reduction *)

Lemma value_no_step : forall v t, value v -> ~ step v t.
Proof.
  intros v t Hv. induction Hv; intro Hstep; inversion Hstep.
Qed.

Lemma value_lc : forall v, value v -> locally_closed v.
Proof. intros v Hv. destruct Hv; auto using lc_true, lc_false. Qed.

Lemma sn_value : forall v, value v -> strongly_normalizing v.
Proof.
  intros v Hv. constructor. intros t' Hstep.
  exfalso. eapply value_no_step; eassumption.
Qed.
```

Next, the logical relation.

```coq
(** ** The logical relation *)

Fixpoint V (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => value t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\
      (exists T' t1, t = tm_abs T' t1 /\
        (forall w, V T1 w ->
           strongly_normalizing (open t1 w) /\
           (forall u, multi step (open t1 w) u -> value u -> V T2 u)))
  end.

Definition E (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall u, multi step t u -> value u -> V T u).
```

```coq
Lemma E_intro : forall T t,
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  (value t -> V T t) ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> E T t') ->
  E T t.
Proof.
  intros T t Hsn Hv Hs. split; [exact Hsn|].
  intros u Hm Hval.
  destruct Hm as [| t0 y u0 Hstep Hrest].
  - apply Hv. exact Hval.
  - eapply E_multi_V; [exact Hrest | apply Hs; exact Hstep | exact Hval].
```

Now the `E_if`:
```coq
Lemma sn_if : forall t1 t2 t3,
  strongly_normalizing t1 -> strongly_normalizing t2 -> strongly_normalizing t3 ->
  strongly_normalizing (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 t3 H1.
  enough (Hgen : forall t1, strongly_normalizing t1 ->
     forall t2 t3, strongly_normalizing t2 -> strongly_normalizing t3 ->
       strongly_normalizing (tm_if t1 t2 t3)).
  { apply Hgen; assumption. }
  intros t1 Hsn1. induction Hsn1 as [t1 Hfun IH]. intros t2 t3 H2 H3.
  constructor. intros u Hstep. inversion Hstep; subst.
  - exact H2.
  - exact H3.
  - apply IH; [eassumption | exact H2 | exact H3].
Qed.

Lemma E_if : forall T t1 t2 t3,
  E Ty_Bool t1 -> E T t2 -> E T t3 -> E T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
Proof.
  intros T t1 t2 t3 HE1 HE2 HE3.
  enough (Hgen : forall t1, strongly_normalizing t1 -> E Ty_Bool t1 ->
     forall t2 t3, E T t2 -> E T t3 -> E T (tm_if t1 t2 t3)).
  { apply Hgen; [exact (proj1 HE1) | exact HE1 | exact HE2 | exact HE3]. }
  intros t1 Hsn1. induction Hsn1 as [t1 Hfun IH]. intros HE1 t2 t3 HE2 HE3.
  apply E_intro.
  - apply sn_if; [exact (proj1 HE1) | exact (proj1 HE2) | exact (proj1 HE3)].
  - intros Hv; inversion Hv.
  - intros u Hstep; inversion Hstep; subst.
    + exact HE2.
    + exact HE3.
    + apply IH; [eassumption | eapply E_step_fwd; eassumption | exact HE2 | exact HE3].
Qed.
```

Now the fundamental theorem:
```coq
Definition sem_env (Gamma : context) (rho : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (rho x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> E T (rho x)).

Lemma subst_open_comm : forall rho x w t k,
  (forall y, locally_closed (rho y)) ->
  locally_closed w ->
  ~ In x (fvs t) ->
  subst_f (extend rho x w) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) =
  open_rec k w (subst_f rho t).
Proof.
  intros rho x w t. induction t; intros k Hlc Hw Hfresh; simpl in *; auto.
  - (* bvar n *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E; auto.
    apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n.
    unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.
  - (* fvar a *) 
    destruct (Nat.eqb x a) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst a. exfalso. apply Hfresh. simpl. left. reflexivity.
    + apply open_rec_lc. apply Hlc.
  - (* app *) rewrite IHt1, IHt2; auto.
  - (* abs *) simpl. f_equal. apply IHt; auto.
  - (* if *) rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3; auto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, this needs care with the simplification behavior. Let me think through each case after `simpl in *`:

Goal (bvar n): `subst_f (extend rho x w) (if k =? n then tm_fvar x else tm_bvar n) = open_rec k w (subst_f rho (tm_bvar n))`.

Hmm, actually `open_rec k (tm_fvar x) (tm_bvar n)` simplifies to `(if k =? n then tm_fvar x else tm_bvar n)` (since open_rec is a Fixpoint on t; with t = tm_bvar n, it reduces to the if, but the if is on `Nat.eqb k n`, not reducible). Then subst_f applied to that if-expression: subst_f matches on its argument which is an if — `simpl` will get stuck (can't reduce subst_f applied to a match on a non-constructor). So `simpl` gives: `subst_f (extend rho x w) (if k =? n then tm_fvar x else tm_bvar n) = (if k =? n then w else tm_bvar n)`.

```
  - (* bvar *) simpl. destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E; simpl.
```

Hmm. Right — the lemma `open_rec_lc : forall t n u, lc_at n t -> open_rec n u t = t` needs lc_at n t, but we have lc_at 0 (rho a). Lemma lc_at_mono : forall n m t, n <= m -> lc_at n t -> lc_at m t.

```
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall n m t, n <= m -> lc_at n t -> lc_at m t.
```

```
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall t n m, lc_at n t -> n <= m -> lc_at m t.
Proof.
  induction t; intros n m Hlc Hle; inversion Hlc; subst; auto using lc_fvar, lc_true, lc_false.
  - apply lc_bvar. (* n0 < n <= m *) 
     eapply Nat.lt_le_trans; eassumption.
  - apply lc_app; [apply IHt1 with n | apply IHt2 with n]; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IHt with (n := S n). assumption. (* S n <= S m *) 
     apply Nat.succ_le_mono; assumption.
  - apply lc_if; [apply IHt1 with n | apply IHt2 with n | apply IHt3 with n]; assumption.
Qed.
```

Now the fundamental theorem:

```coq
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, <{ Gamma |-- t \in T }> ->
  forall rho, sem_env Gamma rho -> E T (subst_f rho t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty. induction Hty; intros rho Hsem.
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply (proj2 Hsem). exact H.
  - (* T_Abs *) 
    simpl. split.
    + apply sn_abs.
    + intros u Hm Hvalu.
      (* need V (T1->T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst_f rho t1)) *)
      split.
      * (* lc *) apply value_lc in Hvalu. exact Hvalu.
      * exists T1, (subst_f rho t1). split; [reflexivity|].
```

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x)) as HE.
  assert (Henv : sem_env empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intro x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x S Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate. }
  specialize (HE Henv).
  rewrite subst_f_id in HE.
  apply E_sn in HE. exact HE.
Qed.
```

```coq

(* ============================================================ *)
(* Auxiliary developments for the strong normalization proof.    *)
(* ============================================================ *)

(** Free variables of a term. *)

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

(** Maximum of a list of atoms. *)

Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_spec : forall L x, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list ys); [apply IH; assumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). intro Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_spec L (S (max_list L)) Hin) as H.
  apply Nat.nle_succ_diag_l in H. exact H.
Qed.
```

Then substitution, lc monotonicity, etc.

I'm a bit worried about `fresh_atom`'s proof: `pose proof (max_list_spec L (S (max_list L)) Hin) as H` gives H : S (max_list L) <= max_list L. `apply Nat.nle_succ_diag_l in H` — Nat.nle_succ_diag_l : forall n, ~ S n <= n. To apply it to H, Coq needs to match H's statement `S (max_list L) <= max_list L` with `S ?n <= ?n` → n := max_list L ✓. Then H : False ✓ exact H.
